#5280. ApeChain migrates to Chainlink CCIP for APE and USDC transfers – Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

ApeChain is replacing LayerZero with Chainlink CCIP. This move comes as the layer 3 blockchain aims to strengthen its infrastructure and improve connectivity across the Yuga Labs ecosystem.

The migration makes CCIP the official cross-chain standard for ApeChain, Bored Ape Yacht Club applications, and APE.

Additionally, it will be migrating the ChainLink Cross-Chain Token (CCT) that is used by the CCT implementation of APE and USD Coin [USDC], among others. This is to enable programmable transfers from these tokens without having to use LayerZero’s former OFT.

Therefore, this may provide easier access to the decentralized application and community space across the Ethereum [ETH], Arbitrum, Base, and Polygon chains. As well, Chainlink has described CCIP as a single secure cross-chain standard.

The expanded reach of Chainlink CCIP provides a larger scale significance for the ApeChain integration. Chainlink CCIP processes over $25 billion in cumulative transfer volume.

It also currently supports 60 to 77 different networks and has sent over 1.63 million messages. Previously, it was integrated with BitGo’s WBTC, Wyoming’s FRNT, and other assets from Kelp and Mantle.

These previous integrations have moved over $15 billion worth of token value via CCIP in 2026. In addition to providing additional support to this expanding network of users, Yuga Labs will also bring a large, consumer-focused, and NFT-centered ecosystem into play.

Ultimately, Rising transfers, repeat activity, deeper liquidity, and competitive fees will show whether adoption delivers lasting growth.

While CCIP is continuing to expand its reach, it needs to continue translating this reach into sustainable revenue. More importantly, CCIP recently underwent its first upgrade (CCIP 2.0) since September 2026.

Additionally, integrations with Aave [AAVE], Lombard, and Venus demonstrate CCIP’s continued involvement in the development of DeFi. While CCIP continues to add to its involvement in DeFi infrastructure, commercial returns for the project remain limited.

This gap represents the difficulty in transitioning from simply being involved in infrastructure adoption into generating consistent economic returns.

ApeChain has adopted Chainlink CCIP as its cross-chain standard for APE and other ecosystem assets.

Chainlink’s CCIP adoption is growing, but low fee revenue remains a challenge.

#5279. Grok AI Predicts TRUMP Crypto to Zero as Democrats Turn the US Blue

Elon Musk’s Grok AI predicts a price target for the TRUMP crypto memecoin that would see the President’s token utterly worthless

The Official TRUMP memecoin is positioned for a severe and sustained collapse, Elon Musk’s Grok AI predicts. In the 2026 midterms, Democrats delivered a sweeping victory, reclaiming control of both the House and the Senate. The party flipped multiple Republican-held Senate seats, including in traditionally red or competitive states, and secured a clear House majority.

This outcome sharply rebukes the current Republican administration and significantly weakens the party’s legislative influence for the remainder of the presidential term. With Democrats now holding both chambers, Republicans’ ability to advance their agenda is heavily constrained, and the balance of power in Washington has shifted decisively.

(SOURCE: Grok AI Predicts a Catastrophic TRUMP Crypto Crash)

Democratic majorities have historically favored stronger regulatory oversight of digital assets. A more assertive stance on crypto, ranging from expanded enforcement to potentially restrictive legislation, would remove much of the political tailwind that previously supported Trump-branded tokens.

Political memecoins like TRUMP are especially vulnerable: their value is tightly linked to narrative momentum and perceived political relevance. A cooler regulatory climate and diminished Republican influence could accelerate capital flight from these assets and pressure the more speculative corners of the broader crypto market.

TRUMP has already experienced a dramatic drawdown from its early 2025 peak near $75 and currently trades at a small fraction of that level, down -15% on the day and -98% from that $75 all-time high.

Price action shows persistent lower highs, weak follow-through on any short-term rallies, and elevated sensitivity to political headlines, sparking fear among the remaining TRUMP crypto holders.

Volume has tended to expand more on downside moves than on recoveries, a classic sign of distribution. With a large portion of the supply still subject to unlocks and concentrated holdings, the technical structure remains fragile.

A break of recent support levels would likely trigger further liquidation and accelerate the downtrend. With reduced political relevance, rising regulatory pressure, and deteriorating technicals, the path of least resistance points sharply lower.

A Democratic-led Congress is more likely to favor stricter oversight of digital assets. This environment reduces political support for crypto-friendly initiatives and raises the prospect of tighter regulation or enforcement.

Among all crypto assets, the Official TRUMP memecoin stands out as uniquely exposed. Its value is closely tied to political momentum and the current administration’s standing.

As the most visible Trump-branded token, it is expected to attract disproportionate attention from regulators, critics, and risk-averse investors. While the broader market may feel some pressure, TRUMP is positioned to suffer more severe and prolonged selling than most other tokens.

By January 1, 2027, Official TRUMP could trade in the $0.08 – $0.25 range, with a severe downside scenario testing levels below $0.05.

#5278. Ripple CEO Sees Bipartisan Crypto Support After U.S. Midterm Elections, Renews Call for Clear Rules

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse has expressed confidence in the crypto industry’s political position after the November 3 U.S. midterm elections, citing victories by pro-crypto candidates from both major parties.

However, despite Democrats securing control of Congress and industry-backed political committees suffering several expensive defeats, Garlinghouse believes efforts to establish clear digital asset regulations in the country remain on track.

In a Wednesday post on X, Garlinghouse highlighted the election victories of lawmakers supportive of the crypto industry, arguing that political backing for digital assets extends beyond party lines.

“Pro-crypto members won on both sides of the aisle….and the 67M (and growing) Americans using crypto aren’t going anywhere. New Congress, with the same mission: clear rules, built in America,” he wrote.

Meanwhile, the industry is assessing the election’s impact on crypto regulation. Despite Democrats gaining control of both chambers, Ripple believes bipartisan support for digital assets will keep efforts to secure clear rules moving forward.

Ripple Chief Legal Officer Stuart Alderoty also weighed in on the election outcome, rejecting suggestions that the results represented a major defeat for the crypto industry.

Congratulating the newly elected lawmakers, Alderoty said Ripple was prepared to work with representatives from both parties to maintain America’s position as a leading destination for blockchain and digital asset businesses.

He highlighted Fairshake’s performance, a crypto-backed political action committee that supported candidates during the election cycle. According to Alderoty, the organization secured meaningful victories in both primary contests and the general election.

He further referenced preliminary figures from the Stand With Crypto initiative, which indicated that a substantial number of pro-crypto lawmakers had secured seats across party lines.

“Let me be clear — some headlines will call this a setback for crypto. The facts are far more nuanced,” Alderoty stated.

He attributed the outcome to years of political engagement involving grassroots supporters, industry organizations and advocacy groups. The CLO believes these efforts have helped establish relationships with lawmakers who could play an important role in shaping future digital asset legislation.

Beyond the election results, the Ripple executive also emphasized the growing number of Americans participating in the crypto economy.

According to Alderoty, more than 67 million Americans use digital assets for activities ranging from building financial independence to running businesses and making payments.

Additionally, banks and businesses are increasingly adopting crypto-related services, reflecting the industry’s growing presence in mainstream finance.

For Ripple, this growing adoption reinforces the need for legislation that gives businesses and consumers a clearer understanding of the rules governing digital assets.

Politically, the election results were mixed for the crypto industry, particularly for organizations that spent heavily supporting pro-crypto candidates.

According to Reuters, Fairshake-backed candidates had won 29 of the 34 races decided as of its November 4 report. The committee supported 32 House candidates, including 19 Republicans and 13 Democrats.

However, the industry also suffered notable defeats despite its considerable campaign spending.

One of the biggest setbacks came in Ohio, where Democrat Sherrod Brown won a Senate seat despite crypto-backed political committees spending more than $60 million to defeat him.

Overall, crypto interests spent about $200 million during the election cycle, highlighting the industry’s growing involvement in U.S. politics and its determination to influence the direction of digital asset regulation.

#5277. UPay to Add On-chain Risk Screening with WIDTH’s DART

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

Sara is steadily working on cryptocurrency evaluations, news, and fluctuations in digital currency prices. She is guest author associated with many cryptocurrencies admin and contributes as an active guide to readers about recent updates on virtual currencies.

UPay will add on-chain risk screening through a partnership with WIDTH, a compliance and risk technology provider. Under the agreement, announced on 6 November, the digital asset payment platform will use WIDTH’s DART to screen wallet addresses and transactions and give reviewers more detail on what triggered a risk flag.

Digital asset payments are bringing on-chain funds into everyday spending, which can make risk reviews more complex. Reviewers may need to check who the customer is, which wallet addresses were involved and how the funds moved.

DART’s wallet address risk screening (KYA) can identify links between an address and high-risk entities. Its transaction risk screening (KYT) examines where funds came from and where they went. This allows reviewers to trace each result back to the screening criteria and transaction records behind it.

“Our focus is on making risk findings useful to the people reviewing them,” said Chionh Chye Kit, Co-Founder and CEO of WIDTH. “Scores, matched rules and transaction paths help explain why an address or transaction needs attention.”

UPay will use the screening results to support manual reviews and follow-up under its own risk policies.

“Our aim is to give reviewers more than a risk flag,” said Owen Yang, CEO of UPay. “We want them to understand what triggered it and see the transaction history behind it, so they can decide what needs further investigation.”

Screening requirements can vary by product, how funds move and where a service is offered. UPay and WIDTH will agree which payment processes DART will cover and how the findings will feed into reviews. The scope will depend on supported assets, blockchain networks and business arrangements, with both companies evaluating DART’s use based on feedback.

Following the partnership, DART’s findings will supplement UPay’s own customer due diligence and independent review. However, UPay remains responsible for its own business decisions and for meeting applicable regulatory obligations.

Founded in 2023, UPay is a financial technology company that offers digital wallets, payment cards, OTC services, crypto payments, cross-border settlement, remittance and enterprise APIs.

#5276. Circle reports $865M Q3 revenue – Can USDC challenge Tether’s dominance?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Circle Internet Group (CRCL) reported strong financial performance in Q3 2026, with growth in USDC adoption, transaction activity, revenue, and profitability compared to the same period last year.

As per the financial report, the average amount of USDC in circulation reached $73.3 billion, increasing 8% year-over-year. Meanwhile, USDC’s on-chain transaction volume surged 49% to $13.9 trillion.

At the same time, the company also recorded $865 million in total revenue and reserve income, marking a 17% annual increase. This includes income earned from investing the reserves backing USDC, primarily in cash and short-term U.S. government securities.

On the financial front, Circle generated $658 million in reserve income, down 8% from the previous year. This decline was primarily attributed to a lower reserve return rate of 3.5%, compared to the previous year’s rate, which was 61 basis points higher.

In simple terms, lower returns on the assets backing USDC affected the company’s primary revenue source. However, other revenue increased significantly to $207 million, largely due to the ARC token presale, which contributed $182 million.

All of this happened as Circe went through major developments in Q3 2026. Starting with the launch of Arc’s public mainnet on the 16th of September 2026, which is Circle’s Layer-1 blockchain designed to support financial applications, payments, and institutional transactions. Then Circle also expanded its global payments ecosystem through several partnerships.

The company signed a definitive agreement to acquire Singapore-based cross-border payments platform Tazapay, a deal expected to close in 2027, subject to regulatory approvals. Binance also announced a $100 million equity investment in Circle and renewed its strategic partnership to promote USDC adoption.

Meanwhile, Visa moved its USDC-based stablecoin prefunding solution from pilot testing to commercial deployment, while Samsung Wallet introduced USDC-based international payment capabilities for eligible US users. All of this coincided with Circles’ stock trading at $76.12 after a hike of 1.17% in the past trading session.

Yet, despite all of this, Tether’s USDT still continues to account for the largest share of overall stablecoin transaction activity. As per data from Visa on-chain analytics, USDT recorded $763.77 billion from July to September 2026 as compared to USDC, which recorded $123.73 billion during the same period.

In fact, AMBCrypto also reported that Q4 has started on a weak note, with the stablecoin market cap struggling to cross $310 billion, suggesting limited liquidity across the crypto market.

At the same time, the European Banking Authority (EBA) has designated Circle’s USDC as the first major e-money token under the EU’s MiCA framework. Starting 12th November, 2026, Circle will face stricter supervisory requirements, with the EBA monitoring its capital, reserves, liquidity, and redemption guarantees.

#5275. Litecoin Takes Savage Swipe at XRP With Controversial Post That Has Crypto Community Talking— Here’s What Happened

Litecoin has reignited its long-running rivalry with XRP after taking another provocative jab at the Ripple-linked cryptocurrency in a post that quickly caught the crypto community’s attention. This time, the Litecoin account joked that it could no longer use a controversial term when referring to XRP, prompting fresh reactions from the XRPArmy. The remark has added another twist to the ongoing rivalry between the two crypto communities.

In the Nov. 5 post, Litecoin wrote: “Me finding out I can’t use the word retarded for XRP anymore.” The remark seemed to poke fun at XRP while referencing restrictions on the offensive term, adding another provocative moment to the rivalry between the two crypto communities.

One XRP supporter was quick to fire back, questioning Litecoin’s standing in the crypto market and accusing it of underperforming against other established cryptocurrencies for years. The user argued that Litecoin had little room to mock XRP while Zcash had moved far ahead of it

The XRP supporter’s criticism also reflects a notable difference in the two assets’ recent market performance. Zcash (ZEC) has surged approximately 138% in 2026, while Litecoin (LTC) has fallen around 30% year-to-date, according to comparative market data. Litecoin has also lagged behind XRP in annualized returns over the past five years, giving the supporter’s argument some market context.

Another XRP supporter joined the discussion, questioning Litecoin’s relevance and taking a swipe at its real-world adoption, asking: “Has anyone used Litecoin in the past 10 years?”

The latest jab is not the first time Litecoin has taken aim at XRP and its community. The rivalry became particularly heated in August 2025, when Litecoin’s official X account published a lengthy, sarcastic post disguised as a “fun fact.” The message mocked XRP’s ambitions in the banking sector and ridiculed the narrative that Ripple’s payment infrastructure could transform traditional finance.

The post also took a personal swipe at Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse, calling him “Brad Garlicmouse” in a satirical remark. It further questioned the value of XRP’s banking-related use cases and drew criticism from members of the XRP community.

The latest back-and-forth comes as both cryptocurrencies continue to trade significantly below their previous all-time highs. Litecoin is hovering around $55.57, approximately 86.5% below its 2021 peak, while XRP trades at $1.31 after losing roughly 64% of its value since hitting its $3.65 all-time high. Despite the sharp corrections, both assets remain in the spotlight as their communities debate their market performance and long-term relevance.

#5274. PEARL integrates with NEAR Intents to expand cross-chain access for AI miners — Can demand follow?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Pearl, the native token of the Pearl Layer-1 network, has launched on NEAR Intents. The move allows for swapping of tokens across more than 35 different networks. Additionally, the integration provides the Near Protocol’s [NEAR] Click API.

As a result, this makes it possible for wallets and applications to integrate Pearl [PEARL] token swaps.

In simple words, through NEAR’s infrastructure, miners will be able to swap PEARL for assets within all of those 35+ networks. Therefore, there may be easier access to stablecoins or other currencies for miners as they pay for electricity and hardware.

More importantly, Pearl’s Proof of Useful Work model links token issuance to AI computations that also secure the network. As such, miners receive both block rewards and inference fees based upon the same workload they are using to mine blocks.

This link could create a direct relationship between AI usage and token utility. However, for PEARL to have sustained value, it will require consistent AI-related workloads creating sufficient activity and generating fees that can support miners.

As part of the integration, NEAR intents provides PEARL with access to an existing cross-chain market. No additional cross-chain bridges for each individual network would be required for this.

Currently, NEAR intents’ ecosystem provides a cross-chain marketplace that includes approximately 38 networks, along with more than 200 assets. Major stablecoin markets are included in the asset list as well.

This is important for miners who need to have liquid means to exit transactions quickly in order to meet costs associated with electricity and hardware. NEAR intents reported more than $37 billion in cumulative volume and more than $144 million in daily volume traded.

A large trading platform like this offers stability for those looking to utilize cross-chain swaps.

Moreover, confidential transactions enable users to transfer funds anonymously. Although the fact that the entire network is utilizing this function does not necessarily mean that there will be a greater demand for PEARL.

Instead, it will rely upon how frequently miners and traders utilize these services to conduct swaps, develop deeper liquidity levels, and ensure reliable execution.

PEARL’s next challenge is converting wider access into sustained market demand. PEARL’s market activity will show whether easier cross-chain access translates into genuine demand. At press time, the token was trading around $1.69, up 17% in the last 24 hours.

The daily volume increased alongside price rally, hovering near $12 million and was up 36%.

However, activity remains concentrated on a few centralized exchanges, suggesting NEAR access has yet to reshape its liquidity. This matters because GPU miners need reliable markets to convert rewards into operating funds.

Ultimately, stronger demand must therefore come from real AI workloads and paid inference, not token issuance alone.

PEARL (PEARL) has joined NEAR Intents, gaining confidential swap access across more than 35 blockchain networks.

PEARL’s long-term demand will depend on real AI workloads, paid inference, and sustained trading activity.

#5273. Bitcoin Falls Below $76,000 as Chip Stocks Crash and Liquidations Hit $1 Billion: Why Is Crypto Down Today?

Why is crypto down today? A chip-stock crash sent Bitcoin below $76K, wiped out $1B in longs and pulled $430M from Bitcoin ETFs.

Short answer to “why is crypto down today?” Bitcoin fell 6% to below $76,000 after a Wall Street chip-stock crash on Thursday, November 5, wiped out $1.03 billion in leveraged bets and pulled $430 million out of Bitcoin ETFs. The selling hasn’t stopped overnight.

Bitcoin traded at about $75,700 on Binance on Friday morning, down from $80,547 at the start of Thursday. Ethereum lost 8.2% to $2,218, XRP fell 9.7% to $1.18 and Solana dropped 6.9% to $89.22. The Crypto Fear & Greed Index fell to 28, in “Fear”, from a neutral 50 a day earlier.

The trigger came from stocks. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index closed down 12.2%, its members Nvidia and AMD lost 9.3% and 17.3%, and the Nasdaq fell 5.5%. Crypto stocks went with them: Strategy dropped 9.3% and Coinbase 9.7%.

So what turned a tech sell-off into a crypto crash, and where can Bitcoin find a floor?

Today was a 4σ day for equities. The S&P 500 fell 4.4% and the Nasdaq fell 5.5%, a decline seen roughly once every 19 months. Bitcoin fell about 5%. $STRC closed at $99.56, just 44 cents below par.

— Michael Saylor (@saylor) November 5, 2026

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

Wall Street opened at 14:30 UTC, and Bitcoin broke within the hour. The 15:00 UTC candle on Binance fell from $78,288 to $75,568, the day’s busiest hour with $513 million traded.

Yahoo Finance’s market coverage linked the stock rout to the Democrats’ clear midterm win and what it could mean for AI infrastructure spending and its regulation. Chipmakers, the biggest winners of the AI trade, took the hardest hit.

Leverage did the rest. CoinGlass counted $1.03 billion in liquidations over 24 hours, positions closed by force when margin ran out. Longs made up $924.94 million of that, or 89.4%, across 199,365 traders.

Bitcoin longs lost $255.88 million and Ether longs $163.83 million. The biggest single wipeout was a $10.94 million ETHUSDT position on Binance.

DISCOVER: Trump Media Moves $344 Million in Bitcoin to Crypto.com as TRUMP Hits a Record Low

US spot Bitcoin ETFs saw $430.2 million in net outflows on Thursday, and not one of the 12 funds took in money. SoSoValue’s ETF tracker shows Fidelity’s FBTC led with $199.87 million out, ahead of BlackRock’s IBIT at $104.97 million.

Grayscale’s GBTC lost $49.90 million, Franklin Templeton’s EZBC $32.92 million, VanEck’s HODL $23.64 million and ARKB $18.90 million.

It undid Wednesday in one session. Funds had added $74.6 million on November 4, the only inflow day so far this month. November’s first four trading days now add up to $869.8 million in outflows, on top of $515.9 million that left in October.

Fidelity’s turn is the detail to watch. FBTC took in $236.7 million on Wednesday while IBIT bled, then gave back most of it a day later.

Bitcoin closed Thursday at $76,088, just under its 200-day EMA at $76,536. That average had held as support through the October pullback.

The 50-day EMA at $79,822 now sits above the price, and the $81,971 high from before last week’s Binance hack is the next resistance beyond it.

On the downside, the $74,968 swing low is the first support. Below it, the 50% Fibonacci retracement of the July-to-September rally sits at $72,598.

One caveat: funding rates on Bitcoin perpetuals are still slightly positive on OKX, so longs haven’t fully cleared out even after a billion-dollar flush.

For anyone still asking “why is crypto down today?”, the answer runs through Nasdaq. If chip stocks steady when New York opens on Friday, Bitcoin has a shot at reclaiming its 200-day average. If they don’t, $74,968 is the line between a bad day and a deeper correction.

#5272. Polygon eyes hard fork to recover $6.5 mln in stolen POL – But at what cost?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

In a surprising development, Polygon has proposed a major network upgrade to recover approximately 70.49 million POL tokens, worth around $6.5 million. These were those tokens that were stolen during two separate security incidents involving Polymarket and Binance in October 2026.

The proposal aims to move the stolen tokens from five attacker-controlled wallets into recovery wallets designated by the compromised companies.

5 attacker-controlled addresses hold about 70.49M POL: about 23.86M POL (4 addresses) from the Polymarket incident and about 46.63M POL (1 address) from the Binance incident. The funds cannot move, but nothing in the current setup lets them be returned either.

Polygon’s block producers have already added these addresses to their transaction blocklists. This they did to prevent the attackers from moving the funds further. However, this measure only freezes the assets, and it does not return them to their actual owners.

The existing system also requires block producers to restart their infrastructure whenever blocklist changes are made, making it difficult to maintain these restrictions over time.

Hence, to address this issue, Polygon has proposed a hard fork, which is a major network upgrade that changes the blockchain’s underlying rules.

Under the proposal, at a predetermined block, the POL balances of the five identified wallets would be reduced to zero. The same amounts would then be credited to recovery addresses verified and designated by Polymarket and Binance.

However, for this plan to succeed, the proposal also requires both companies to prove ownership of their designated recovery addresses before the upgrade. If approved, developers will first implement and test the changes on Polygon’s Amoy testnet before deploying them on mainnet.

At the same time, validators, exchanges, and node operators will also need to upgrade their software before activation. However, the proposal has raised important questions about decentralization and user ownership.

Normally, blockchain transactions cannot be reversed or wallet balances moved without the owner’s private keys. But Polygon’s proposed intervention would override this principle through a consensus-level change.

It is an extreme action that should not be reproduced commonly.

This comes as the Polygon’s MATIC was trading at $0.098 after a hike of 2.08% in the past 24 hours.

But with 21Shares recently withdrawing approximately $11 million worth of POL from Coinbase over the past 30 days, including 45.7 million POL worth $4.08 million on 28th October, concerns remain.

#5271. ECB Links Europe’s T+1 Securities Transition to a Broader Settlement Overhaul

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: European Central Bank officials have discussed the planned move to T+1 securities settlement in October 2027 alongside longer-term work on tokenized settlement infrastructure. A future regulatory settlement date should not be confused with a finished network rollout.

The deadline matters most to firms that process transactions across several jurisdictions. They have to harmonise instructions, reconcile holdings and manage currencies while the clock gets shorter. Blockchain advocates should explain specifically which tasks become easier and which stay with regulated intermediaries.

European Central Bank officials have discussed the planned move to T+1 securities settlement in October 2027 alongside longer-term work on tokenized settlement infrastructure. A speech published October 29 outlined how shortening the conventional settlement cycle and experimenting with new technology fit into wider financial-market modernisation. The two projects are related strategic topics, not a statement that blockchain technology will automatically replace Europe’s existing settlement systems next year. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

T+1 means most eligible securities trades settle one business day after the trade date, compared with a longer conventional cycle. Shorter cycles can reduce outstanding exposures and certain capital needs, but they compress the time available to resolve breaks, fund positions and coordinate across time zones. Tokenization addresses a different design question: how ownership records and money can move using new technical infrastructure.

The connection is operational discipline. A market trying to settle sooner needs robust automation, accurate records and predictable funding. Tokenized assets may eventually support more tightly connected workflows, but they are not necessary to achieve the near-term T+1 timetable. Existing post-trade institutions and banks still have crucial responsibilities.

A tokenized instrument must connect technical records with enforceable financial rights. Trading software can move a token quickly, but an issuer, custodian, transfer agent or settlement bank may still have to recognise the result. This is why tokenization announcements need to explain which part of the lifecycle is changing, not merely advertise that a blockchain is involved.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: DTCC’s tokenized securities infrastructure rollout and tokenized fund redemptions tested over a weekend. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

A future regulatory settlement date should not be confused with a finished network rollout. Different products and markets can have exclusions or distinct implementation rules. Nor should a policy speech be read as authorising every private tokenized security to circulate without existing investor-protection requirements.

Real settlement infrastructure is judged by whether it works on bad days, not just during presentations. Reconciliation, asset eligibility, funding, corporate actions and recovery from errors remain central. Continuous technical operation can improve coordination, yet it does not create market liquidity or remove the obligations of regulated participants.

Watch for detailed industry testing, bank funding arrangements and official updates on both T+1 readiness and central-bank-money experiments. The important measure is whether settlement failures and costs fall without shifting uncontrolled risk to participants. Europe’s timeline creates a concrete deadline, while the tokenization debate remains a longer-term architecture choice.

#5270. Zcash’s Game-Changing NU7 Upgrade Goes Live, Slashing Block Times 3X as $5,000 ZEC Rally Comes Into Focus

NU7 has successfully activated on mainnet. A new era of Zcash is here. $ZEC pic.twitter.com/rPaSFe9h7j

The Network Sustainability Mechanism addresses a long-term challenge that Zcash shares with Bitcoin: how to keep its network secure as mining rewards decline with each halving. As block subsidies become smaller, both networks face questions about whether transaction fees alone can provide enough incentive for miners to continue securing the blockchain.

Under the new mechanism, 60% of transaction fees from each block goes into a publicly visible reserve, while miners receive the remaining 40%. The approach creates a dedicated funding pool to support Zcash’s long-term security and sustainability while preserving its hard-capped supply of 21 million ZEC.

The reserved fees are scheduled to be gradually reissued through block rewards starting at block 8,939,664, expected in 2031, with the recycled funds distributed along a smooth curve on top of the existing halving schedule.

NU7 also introduces a cap on the amount of shielded transaction activity that can be included in each block, reducing the volume of data wallets need to scan when searching for incoming payments. This is expected to make wallet synchronization more efficient and improve the overall user experience.

Despite the major network upgrade, ZEC has yet to register a strong bullish market reaction. The cryptocurrency was down 0.8% over the past 24 hours, trading at $1,313 at press time, as broader weakness across the crypto market weighed on prices.

Despite the recent price pullback, some prominent crypto investors remain bullish on Zcash’s longer-term prospects. Chris Burniske, a renowned investor known for his early interest in Solana (SOL), pointed to the ZEC/BTC chart as the key indicator to watch, describing it as “still the only chart that matters as it shows what comes next.”

#5267. Vitalik Buterin Reveals What Crypto Exchanges Must Do To Survive

Moneycontrol sat down with Ethereum founder Vitalik Buterin for an exclusive conversation on cryptocurrency security following the high-profile Binance breach.

The interviewer asked what guidance he would offer the industry after the incident underscored how even the most advanced exchanges remain exposed to cyberattacks and thefts, particularly as artificial intelligence tools grow more sophisticated.

Buterin replied by first pointing to encouraging trends in the underlying technology before turning to the practical mindset now taking hold across the sector. “The good news is that smart contract hacks have gone down a huge amount. All of the big hacks that we’ve seen over the last one and a half years, they’re much more concentrated in the user interface layer and in centralized components,” he said.

He added that while incidents haven’t disappeared, the foundation of reliable systems keeps strengthening, and leading firms are adjusting their priorities accordingly. “What we’ve been seeing is more and more people are realizing that their main responsibility for the next two years is not to win but to survive. Everyone is kind of figuring out what the concept of bunker mode means for them,” Buterin observed.

The Ethereum creator added that Binance’s decision to develop its own smart-chain infrastructure offers a useful example of how on-chain contracts can provide stronger protections, a direction he expects more platforms to pursue in the months ahead.​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​

In the latest development, Binance continues recovering from the October 29, 2026, $6.3 billion hack, the largest in crypto history and attributed to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group. The exchange has replenished nearly all affected assets from its own reserves, resumed phased withdrawals, and frozen roughly $3 billion of the stolen funds.

Binance founder Changpeng Zhao addressed the breach in a recent X post, stating, “A hack of this scale means serious security failures occurred. No platform is unhackable, but that cannot be an excuse.”

He further noted that certain details remain embarrassing, described the intrusion as a prolonged campaign that targeted numerous employees and systems, and stressed that as a shareholder, he expects the company to stay transparent, accept responsibility, and deliver meaningful security upgrades.​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​​

#5266. SEC Updates Crypto-Asset FAQs as Firms Seek Clarity on Federal Securities Rules

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The SEC Division of Corporation Finance added questions 1.4, 1.5 and 1.6 to its crypto-assets FAQ on November 2, 2026. SEC staff positions may be informative without having the legal force of a statute, judicial judgment or final Commission rule.

This distinction is worth making because crypto regulation often arrives in several formats: speeches, staff statements, enforcement actions, proposals and final rules. They have different implications even when the subject is the same. A dated FAQ update merits attention without being promoted into a sweeping legal turning point.

The SEC Division of Corporation Finance added questions 1.4, 1.5 and 1.6 to its crypto-assets FAQ on November 2, 2026. The new staff answers address governance through foundations or organisations, what counts as control over participation and the difference between a functional network and a decentralised one. The page expressly says its answers represent staff views, have no legal force and have not been approved or disapproved by the Commission. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

A functioning crypto system can have a native asset that users need for programmatic activity without necessarily being decentralised. Under the staff explanation, decentralisation is a distinct question about whether any party or group holds operational, economic or voting control. A foundation or association involved in broad-based governance is not automatically a controlling central party; the actual powers and decision rules matter. This is a narrower, more useful clarification than saying every governance token has received a new legal classification.

The additions give lawyers and builders more concrete language for evaluating governance arrangements. A protocol may distribute votes widely while retaining privileged access, unilateral upgrade powers or concentrated economic influence. The staff view asks analysts to study those powers rather than rely on the mere existence of a formal foundation. That could influence how firms document their networks, although it does not eliminate case-by-case analysis or statutory legal requirements.

Crypto policy updates arrive as proposals, staff explanations, enforcement cases and final rules. Those documents carry different legal weight even when they use similar language. A careful article tells readers who issued the text, when it was published and whether it changes obligations now. That distinction is especially important for financial firms making compliance decisions.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: DTCC’s tokenization service and settlement controls and financial infrastructure and technology policy coverage. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

SEC staff positions may be informative without having the legal force of a statute, judicial judgment or final Commission rule. Investors should also resist the leap from regulatory discussion to token-price predictions. A frequently asked questions page is not an approval notice for any specific exchange, security or stablecoin.

Regulation ultimately affects operational choices: where customer assets are held, who can move them, how decisions are recorded and which risks are disclosed. The market can debate policy direction, but firms still need to map any published guidance to existing duties and the specific activity they carry out. General labels rarely answer those questions.

Watch for direct Commission statements, formal rulemaking notices, court decisions and clarifications that identify the affected transactions. Teams reviewing compliance can use the FAQ alongside primary legal sources, while documenting areas where further advice is necessary. Precision about who issued a document is essential to accurate policy reporting.

#5264. Stellar Activates Protocol 30 Bell Testnet, Begins Testing Quantum-Resistant Signatures

Stellar’s Bell upgrade reached its next major milestone today, according to the data shared by the Stellar Development Foundation. The Bell (Protocol 30 upgrade) went live on the testnet on Tuesday, November 3. The update rolled out smart-account features for enterprises and post-quantum signature verification, enhancing transaction and security efficiency.

As announced today, Stellar activated the Bell upgrade on its testnet (test network) on Nov. 3. Stellar’s move to enable Bell integration on the testnet is a crucial step as it prepares for a Mainnet vote set for Nov. 23.

Bell’s activation on the testnet allows developers to test the upgrade’s capabilities. Through the test environment, developers explore the capabilities of new smart contract features for businesses and the effectiveness of post-quantum signature verification.

The Bell upgrade, also commonly recognized as Protocol 30, aims to improve smart account capabilities for businesses. At the same time, it prepares Stellar’s infrastructure for future security requirements, including quantum-safe signatures and faster ledgers.

Its mainnet launch is scheduled for Nov. 23, subject to community voting, which could shape the future capabilities and direction of Stellar’s blockchain technology. With the upgrade, businesses can use more sophisticated, efficient accounts, while quantum security remains a long-term necessity.

Stellar entered an important phase on Tuesday as the Bell upgrade went live on its testnet. The upgrade represents another step in the blockchain’s commitment to supporting financial utilities and institutional use cases.

The Protocol 30 upgrade seeks to expand what enterprises can do with accounts on the Stellar blockchain and also lays the foundation for quantum-safe digital signatures.

Digital signatures are important for blockchain security as they allow customers to authorize transactions and prove ownership and control of their accounts.

Advancing quantum computing poses a serious threat to cryptographic blockchains. This explains why Stellar is preparing to make its network quantum-resistant to address such potential risks.

#5263. XRP Ledger Welcomes New Euro Stablecoin

The XRP Ledger (XRPL) community has welcomed a new stablecoin to the blockchain. This time, it's via GateHub, which is launching a new euro-pegged stablecoin for blockchain payments that links the blockchain to the traditional European banking network.

As announced in a press release on Nov. 5, GateHub partnered with Slovenian financial services company Dinaro to launch GateHub Euro (EURGH), a MiCA-compliant (European Union's Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation) stablecoin pegged to the euro.

The new stablecoin operates on both the XRP Ledger and Xahau, an alternative blockchain in the larger XRPL ecosystem with smart contract capabilities.

Part of the significance of EURGH is its SEPA (Single Euro Payments Area) integration. With this, GateHub users can make instant euro payments from their XRPL and Xahau wallets to banks in Europe.

GateHub notes that these transactions can take just seconds to complete, linking blockchain assets to the traditional banking world.

GateHub Euro is a stablecoin that is designed to maintain a value of exactly one euro. This means 1 EURGH should equal €1. It is an electronic money token (EMT) under the EU's MiCA regulations.

GateHub Euro is issued by Dinaro, which is an authorized electronic money institution regulated by the Bank of Slovenia.

According to Dinaro's published terms, it will issue EURGH on a 1:1 basis when it receives Euro funds. Holders of the token will be able to redeem EURGH for Euros at face value (subject to proper checks and compliance procedures).

GateHub users can access traditional European payment systems via their digital wallets because of the SEPA integration. This is an alternative to traditional crypto exchanges for converting funds into the traditional banking network.

GateHub Euro is not the first euro-pegged stablecoin to emerge in the crypto space or on the XRP Ledger.

For instance, there's the MiCA-compliant euro stablecoin EURØP from Schuman Financial.

Meanwhile, there's Circle's EURC, which is a euro-pegged stablecoin for payments and trading on multiple blockchains.

And there's EUR CoinVertible (EURCV) from Société Générale-FORGE, a digital asset business unit of French banking giant Société Générale.

#5262. Solana Microscope Offers Open-Source Monitoring for Complex On-Chain Operations

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The Solana Foundation has described Microscope, an open-source monitoring approach for teams managing on-chain programmes and infrastructure. An alert is not a transaction blocker.

Operational tooling rarely generates the excitement of a new token launch, yet it can determine whether institutions trust a network enough to hold meaningful value on it. Teams want evidence that they can detect unexpected actions before the problem expands. Monitoring is one part of that process, not a substitute for preventive controls.

The Solana Foundation has described Microscope, an open-source monitoring approach for teams managing on-chain programmes and infrastructure. The toolkit is designed to help operators inspect instructions, transactions and alerts while connecting observations to common monitoring workflows. It is an engineering tool, not an announcement that every Solana application now receives automatic protection against attacks. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

In a complex blockchain application, important activity can be hidden within technical transaction instructions that a general-purpose dashboard does not explain well. Monitoring tools try to decode those events and route relevant signals to people who can respond. This is useful for treasury movements, contract administration and unusual operational behaviour, but good alerts depend on choosing the right thresholds and maintaining the integrations.

The wider lesson is that on-chain transparency is not the same thing as operational comprehension. Transaction data may be public while a business still struggles to identify why an event matters or who should investigate it. A self-hostable tool can give teams more control over their monitoring stack, provided they manage uptime, access and alert delivery responsibly.

There is a meaningful gap between a fast transaction and a complete financial service. Solana infrastructure can settle digital assets, but an actual payments or securities product must also manage eligibility, legal rights, local currencies and exceptions. The best reporting follows the full workflow rather than assuming the chain’s speed is the entire product.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Solana’s Samsung Wallet rollout and user test and the latest Solana developer and payments coverage. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

An alert is not a transaction blocker. Microscope does not automatically fix a vulnerable programme, reverse an executed transfer or eliminate the need for security audits. False alarms can consume attention, while incomplete alert definitions can miss harmful activity. A monitored environment still requires tested incident procedures and clear accountability.

Distribution can be an advantage for blockchain applications, but the user experience needs to justify a change in behaviour. Developers also need monitoring and controls to support systems that operate continuously. A credible launch is one that describes available features, supported participants and how success will be measured after the initial announcement.

Watch for adoption in real applications, public documentation and integration examples showing how teams respond to alerts. The most useful evidence would involve measured detection speed and fewer unresolved incidents, rather than the number of dashboards deployed. For Solana developers, monitoring belongs alongside code review and permission design as a basic operating requirement.

#5261. ADA Advancing Regulated Asset Tokenization as Brazil’s Petrobras Brings Sustainable Aviation Fuel Certificates to Cardano

Petrobras’ decision to launch its sustainable aviation fuel on the Cardano blockchain highlights ADA’s expanding role in powering next-gen tokenization.

ADA continues to gain popularity in tokenized real-world assets and institutional blockchain adoption. Petrobras’ move to bring sustainable aviation fuel certificates to Cardano demonstrates this.

Brazil’s state-controlled energy company Petrobras has taken a pioneering leap towards the digital revolution by integrating Cardano blockchain technology to track environmental claims associated with sustainable aviation fuel. This is according to the interview shared earlier today by the Cardano Foundation.

As per the broadcast, Brazilian energy giant Petrobras has tapped Cardano to use ADA-based blockchain applications to track environmental claims linked to sustainable aviation fuel. As stated in the podcast, Petrobras, one of Brazil’s largest companies, has officially started using the Carbon blockchain to verify environmental data connected to low-carbon fuels. Announcements made in late September showed that Petrobras was testing the blockchain.

Latest updates indicate that the Brazilian state-controlled oil company is now using Cardano to track sustainability data for its sustainable aviation fuel (SAF). This blockchain application tokenizes environmental attributes (linked to SAF) into digital tokens designed to prevent reuse or duplication, according to a Wednesday report from the Cardano Foundation.

The SAF system tokenizes sustainable aviation fuel’s environmental attributes on Cardano, turning its sustainability claims into verifiable proof. As a result, the SAF tokens, which represent the fuel’s environmental attributes, prevent the same claim from being used more than once. This explains how the application solves the double-counting problem in carbon markets, enhancing efficiency, trust, transparency, and scalability.

By tracking fuel across production, shipment, and utility, the SAF system improves supply-chain trackability and emissions reporting. Through this innovative approach, the Petrobras aviation project helps prevent multiple firms from claiming ownership of emission-reduction rights associated with using sustainable fuel.

The launch represents another of Cardano’s largest public-sector deployments and expands its utility beyond crypto and DeFi into government tokenization and greenwashing in the aviation industry.

Chairing the Senate Banking Committee would also give Warren more power to conduct investigations and issue subpoenas.

In a Sept. 15 Senate speech, she argued that the U.S. should regulate crypto but opposed crypto bills designed to benefit the industry.

Should Warren take over the Banking Committee in January 2027, she will have much more say in which crypto proposals move forward and which industry executives are grilled.

#5257. Justin Sun Warns Quantum Threat Could Hit Crypto Within 2 Years as TRON Pursues Resistance

Quantum computing is advancing faster than many expect, TRON CEO Justin Sun says. While most people believe quantum computing is still far away, Justin Sun today said crypto attacks are looming.

In an interview organized by FINTECH.TV, Justin Sun said the dangers posed by quantum computers are real and could affect cryptocurrencies within the next year or two. The TRON CEO anticipates that breakthroughs in quantum computing infrastructure are making machines more powerful. This means computers’ ability to break cryptographic systems is becoming more imminent.

The executive recognized potential dangers to public-key cryptography, the backbone of blockchain networks. Crypto networks such as Bitcoin, Ethereum, and many others rely on cryptographic signatures like ECDSA, which are more vulnerable. According to Sun, advancing, powerful, fault-tolerant quantum computers could soon use Shor’s algorithm to break cryptographic signatures more easily than people think.

Currently, most crypto assets are kept in addresses with exposed public keys, putting them at risk of quantum computer attacks. Sun warns the imminent threat is likely within two years, as attackers can collect encrypted data now and decrypt it as quantum computing technology advances.

Today’s quantum computers can’t break cryptographic networks. However, the threat is growing as the technology becomes more powerful. This explains why Justin Sun said TRON is working on post-quantum cryptography.

Last month, the network announced that post-quantum cryptography is available on its testnet. The development marked a significant step toward preparing its infrastructure for potential advances in quantum computing.

This commitment matters because experts project that quantum computing could eventually overpower some of the cryptographic methods currently used to safeguard crypto assets. Post-quantum cryptography aims to build security controls created to resist attacks from powerful quantum computers.

#5254. XRP Ledger's Biggest Lending Upgrade Just Got Closer

The XRP Ledger (XRPL) is on the verge of a significant decentralized finance (DeFi) upgrade that could potentially open up new lending possibilities and liquidity for the XRP Ledger network.

According to Vet (@Vet_X0), a notable XRPL validator and community member, the much-anticipated Lending Protocol amendment has now gained two more Yes votes from validators, moving one step closer to activation.

The Lending Protocol amendment now boasts 21 Yes votes from a total of 35 trusted validators (60%). The amendment needs eight more Yes votes to reach the minimum threshold of 29 validators.

Reaching this threshold doesn’t mean that the upgrade will instantly go live. Instead, reaching the supermajority threshold will initiate the mandatory two-week activation period, which requires a minimum of 80% of validators to support the amendment.

The upgrade, known as XLS-66, is geared towards adding a native lending feature to the XRP Ledger.

In contrast to most crypto lending services, where borrowers are typically required to provide more collateral than the loan amount, XLS-66 is specially crafted to enable fixed-term and non-collateralized lending.

This will enable qualified borrowers to secure loans without the need to hold cryptocurrency collateral that exceeds the loan amount.

According to the official XLS-66 document, the Lending Protocol will enable the pooling of funds via so-called Single Asset Vaults, which can then be loaned to borrowers under set terms.

The proposed lending system involves three key actors: depositors, loan brokers and borrowers.

While depositors are in charge of depositing crypto or supported assets into a vault, loan brokers are tasked with brokering lending deals as well as evaluating borrowers.

For their part, borrowers would receive the loans and pay them back based on the set schedule.

The upgrade could potentially empower financial institutions and other entities to put capital to use on the XRP Ledger, as opposed to just holding or moving around assets.

It may also open up new prospects for earning interest on deposited assets in lending vaults. The amount of interest, however, would largely depend on the actual lending activity and risks.

According to the latest validator data, this amendment has reached 23 out of 35 votes. It still requires six more votes to hit the threshold.

Furthermore, the LendingProtocolV1_1 amendment, which offers upgrades including closed-ended vaults, updated interest accounting, etc., also currently has 21 Yes votes.

All three amendments are currently being considered, and none has yet reached supermajority.

#5253. Bitbase launches ORIGIN, distributing 1M points every week

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Panama City, November 4, 2026 — Bitbase today launched ORIGIN, the first season of its new Bitbase Points program. Ahead of the launch, the digital asset exchange credited points to eligible users who had been trading on the platform, so their past activity counts from day one. The season runs for 26 weeks, until May 4, 2027, with 1,000,000 points distributed each week.

ORIGIN is also the first part of Bitbase 2.0 to reach users directly. Bitbase unveiled 2.0 on November 2, with an upgraded app and website and a new brand identity built around the statement “Market Flows. We Focus.” The idea is that markets will always move, and Bitbase chooses to focus on what lasts for its users. ORIGIN applies the same thinking to participation. Rather than rewarding a single burst of activity, it looks at what users do day after day over six months and credits the contribution they make across that period.

Bitbase built ORIGIN around what users already do on the exchange. Eligible users, new or existing, are enrolled automatically, with no separate registration. Points are allocated daily based on each participant’s contribution, which draws on four sources: trading, assets held on Bitbase, consistent activity and referrals. Because consistency is one of those sources, steady participation adds up over the course of the season.

ORIGIN also rewards users who bring others to Bitbase. A referrer receives an additional 25% of the contribution of each new user they refer directly, while the new user’s own contribution is unaffected.

Starting November 9, participants will also receive a shareable Trading Identity card that reflects their trading habits, with a new card issued every Monday until the season ends.

“Bitbase 2.0 is about focus, and for our users, that means knowing their steady participation is recognized and credited,” said Nicholas Lo, Chief Business Officer at Bitbase. “That’s why ORIGIN started by crediting what users had already done with us. It doesn’t ask anyone to trade differently. It simply records the participation that’s already there, every day, for the next six months.”

Users can track their points and rankings in the Bitbase app and on the website. Full details are available at bitbase.com/points.

#5252. Core Scientific plans to shut down Bitcoin mining despite Q3 revenue hitting $233M

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Core Scientific, once the largest Bitcoin [BTC] miner, has released its Q3 2026 report, but at the same time it has also announced its plans to completely exit Bitcoin mining by the end of 2026.

During its Q3 2026 earnings call, Chief Financial Officer Jim Nygaard said the company expects to complete the mining wind-down in the fourth quarter and enter 2027 as a high-density colocation provider.

This decision came as the firm’s Q3 results were primarily driven by the expansion of its high-density colocation (HDC) business, which supports AI and high-performance computing (HPC) workloads.

Remarking on the same, CEO Adam Sullivan said,

We are nearing full delivery under our first colocation agreement while construction under our second is underway across five campuses.

According to the reports, the company’s colocation revenue surged nearly 60% quarter-over-quarter to $215.4 million, compared to $136.7 million in Q2 2026 and $15 million in Q3 2025.

In fact, its total revenue also increased to $232.9 million, up from $164.2 million in the previous quarter and $81.1 million a year earlier.

As of the 30th of September, Core Scientific was billing for 485 megawatts (MW) of capacity, up from 395 MW in the previous quarter, representing more than $700 million in average annualized colocation revenue.

That said, the company also reported net income of $1.178 billion, compared to a net loss of $1.15 billion in Q2 2026 and $144 million in Q3 2025.

Meanwhile, adjusted EBITDA more than tripled quarter-over-quarter to $133.6 million, with its margin expanding to 57% from 25% in the previous quarter. Core Scientific’s gross profit also climbed to $123.3 million, compared to $70 million in Q2.

However, on the mining side, self-mining revenue declined to $12.3 million from $21.5 million in the previous quarter, while hosted mining revenue stood at $5.17 million. At the same time, revenue from hosting equipment for other Bitcoin miners also declined to $5.2 million from $8.7 million.

For those unaware, Core Scientific began its AI infrastructure transition in 2024 through hosting agreements with CoreWeave, covering 590 MW of capacity, of which 485 MW was billable by September 2026. However, the transition also comes with challenges.

Core Scientific recorded $266.5 million in mining-related asset impairments in Q1 2026 amid weaker Bitcoin prices, low mining profitability, and declining equipment values. It also recorded a $41.9 million loss after terminating mining equipment agreements with Block and Proto in Q2.

That said, Core Scientific is not the only one, as, according to AMBCrypto’s recent report, Bitcoin miners are increasingly selling BTC to fund operations while shifting their infrastructure towards AI and HPC.

This comes as miners swung from a huge inflow spike of about +7K BTC  in early 2026 to an outflow of about -5.7K BTC in early November, while the price slid from around $95K to $75.5K.

However, the latest netflow has settled back to a small positive reading of about 179.4 BTC, which suggests the miner selling pressure has cooled.

#5250. US Voters Back AI Data Center Restrictions in At Least 29 Communities

US Voters Back AI Data Center Restrictions in At Least 29 Communities

Voters in at least 29 U.S. jurisdictions backed bans or restrictions on data-center development Tuesday, according to The Washington Post, dealing a new setback to the massive infrastructure buildout behind artificial intelligence.

The measures ranged from outright prohibitions to requirements for additional public approval. Some were nonbinding advisory votes, meaning their passage does not automatically stop construction.

Ballotpedia tracked 41 data-center measures on November 3 ballots across 10 states. Twenty-eight were framed so that a yes vote would curb development, while 13 would permit or support projects, meaning rejecting one of the latter could also count as a defeat for developers.

Ohio produced some of the clearest results. All 10 charter measures backed by the grassroots group Conserve Ohio passed, according to the Post, and more than three-quarters of voters in Hubbard, Ohio, supported a ban on data centers.

The negative sentiment is so big that voters in Granville, Trenton, and Sunbury backed restrictions with margins of roughly 78% to 82%.

In Illinois, nearly 80% of voters in Joliet Township rejected an advisory proposal supporting AI data centers. In Janesville, Wisconsin, more than 60% approved a requirement for another public vote before major development at a former General Motors site linked to a proposed data center.

These facilities house the chips and servers used to train and run AI systems. Their appetite for electricity has turned proposed campuses into local fights over utility bills and grid capacity, while opponents also raise concerns about water use, noise and deals struck without public scrutiny.

AI companies are aware of the growing backlash and are already working to counter it. Some are stepping up lobbying efforts, while others are turning to public relations campaigns to reshape public perception. OpenAI, for instance, has sought to rebrand data centers as “compute campuses,” in an effort to distance the facilities from the negative connotations increasingly associated with them.

The votes arrived alongside victories by politicians promising tougher oversight of data centers. Democrat Gina Hinojosa defeated Republican Gov. Greg Abbott in Texas after campaigning for a moratorium on new projects, while Ohio's governor-elect Amy Acton also supports a pause and stricter rules.

Industry leaders have warned that blanket restrictions would put U.S. AI investment at risk. BlackRock CEO Larry Fink said last month that a broad moratorium could have serious economic consequences. "If we have a moratorium of some sort in this country, we will be in a recession," he said.

Over the 26-week season, which ends on May 4, 2027, Bitbase will distribute 1,000,000 points each week, allocated daily according to each participant’s contribution.

On November 2, the exchange introduced Bitbase 2.0, updating its brand, app and website under the statement “Market Flows. We Focus.” ORIGIN is the first program to put that statement into practice. While markets move continuously, Bitbase has chosen to assess participation across months rather than individual moments.

“We designed ORIGIN for users who participate consistently, not only during the busiest periods in the market,” said Nicholas Lo, Chief Business Officer at Bitbase. “Over a six-month season, that consistency carries weight. Our responsibility is to make sure every contribution is credited, from the first day of the season to the last.”

#5248. $7.6 Trillion in October: Broadridge’s Distributed Ledger Repo Platform Hits Processing Record

Broadridge Financial Solutions has shared data on the use of its Distributed Ledger Repo (DLR) platform to execute and settle repurchase agreement (repo) transactions. In October 2026, $7.6 trillion in repo transactions were executed using the platform. This represents an average daily volume of $345 billion.

Repo transactions involve the temporary sale of securities with an agreement to repurchase them at a specified price. In practice, repos are a form of short-term borrowing, with the securities serving as collateral. They’re commonly used by financial institutions to raise cash and manage their funding needs.

Broadridge’s DLR platform uses a distributed ledger to execute and settle repo transactions. The company has also recently unveiled a new platform called DLX that can be used to issue, trade, settle, custody, and service tokenized assets throughout their lifecycles.

Broadridge global head of digital innovation Horacio Barakat noted that earlier implementations of blockchain in financial services focused on individual use cases, but now, for tokenized assets to be adopted by banks, broker-dealers, and asset managers, it’s important to think about how they can be implemented within existing workflows and infrastructure. Barakat expects to see more institutions do this in 2027.

It’s worth noting that the $7.6 trillion in repo transactions executed in October is the total notional value of repo transactions during the month. Repo transactions are constantly churning, with new transactions being executed to replace expiring ones.

This data therefore gives a sense of the level of activity in the securities financing market using Broadridge’s DLR platform but doesn’t provide insights into crypto market activity or investor appetite for Bitcoin and Ether.

It also doesn’t provide insights into the amount of new capital flowing into crypto.

To understand the extent to which Broadridge’s DLR platform provides benefits around faster transfer of collateral and settlement, it would be helpful to have data around settlement times and the flow of collateral. Nevertheless, the data does give a sense of the level of securities financing activity using the platform.

#5247. HBAR’s 88% Slide Leaves It Ahead of Most 2021 Rivals

Home » Trendsetter News » HBAR’s 88% Slide Leaves It Ahead of Most 2021 Rivals

A larger token supply complicates any recovery, making a return to its former price a much bigger valuation hurdle.

HBAR remains roughly 88% below its record price, yet the analyst in a recent video places it closer to recovery than most of the largest cryptocurrencies from 2021. That comparison is less a sign of strength than a measure of how severely much of that market has fallen.

Using CoinMarketCap’s November 7, 2021 rankings, the analyst selected the 50 largest coins after excluding stablecoins and wrapped assets. In the video’s snapshot, 41 remained at least 80% below their respective highs, with the median decline around 97%.

“It’s not a forecast,” Cheeky Crypto says. The figures describe historical performance, not the likelihood of a recovery.

At the roughly $0.07 price cited in the video, HBAR would need to rise about eightfold to revisit its approximately $0.57 intraday peak. The analyst separates that brief high from its strongest daily close, just above $0.50 on Binance in September 2021.

The proposed recovery levels start at $0.10, about 43% above the video’s reference price. Next comes $0.25, roughly 3.6 times that price, followed by the record itself.

HBAR has already demonstrated that a substantial rally need not restore earlier buyers’ losses.

Its strongest daily close since 2021 was around $0.37 in January 2025, according to the video, still roughly a quarter below its record close despite an approximately tenfold rise from late 2022.

The bigger obstacle is supply. Cheeky Crypto says the amount of HBAR released from treasury has more than tripled since its record, making a return to $0.57 a materially different valuation proposition.

At the supply level cited, that price would imply a market capitalization near $25 billion. Matching HBAR’s historical peak market capitalization would instead require roughly $0.11 to $0.16 per token, depending on the historical supply figures used.

That valuation is not equivalent to cash that must enter the market, a distinction the analyst explicitly makes. But additional token releases could raise the valuation required to regain the old price.

For investors, the useful distinction is between recovering a token price and recovering a network valuation. HBAR’s smaller drawdown relative to many peers does not erase dilution or make its former high an inevitable destination.

#5246. XRP bear trap alert! Bitfinex shorts surge 652% as supply tightens

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The current market breakdown could go either way.

On one hand, this can lead to a further capitulation phase for the market as key support levels come under threat. However, it could also be seen as a bear trap for those short sellers who have stretched their positions too far. Notably, XRP seems to be at the forefront of this debate.

As the chart below shows, Bitfinex XRP shorts have spiked by 652.89% to 468.2 million. Given the weakening technical momentum, this bearish positioning is not entirely surprising. The altcoin has seen five straight  weekly lows and broken below the $1.20 support level, with its price now approaching the $1 mark.

Meanwhile, XRP’s relative strength is adding another layer of pressure.

After breaking out of its five-week consolidation, the XRP/ETH pair has dropped over 3% this week. This indicates that XRP is losing value to Ethereum, as traders are showing preference towards ETH in this current bear trend. This relative weakness in XRP is therefore contributing to the bearish sentiment.

From a technical standpoint, betting on more Ripple [XRP] downside, with $1 as the next target, looks like a calculated move rather than a random bet. However, on-chain signals are telling a different story, keeping the possibility of a bear trap on the table.

XRP is flashing textbook signs of a potential supply shock.

On the 5th of September, an estimated 13.24 million XRP was taken off exchanges, limiting the amount available to sell (as noted by one analyst). Moreover, whale holdings have increased by nearly 60 million XRP over the past week. While it is unclear whether these are the same tokens that left exchanges, both signals point to growing accumulation by large holders.

The key takeaway? XRP’s Binance Scarcity Index has hit its highest level since 2022. As shown in the chart below, the index has been deeply negative throughout September and October before making a sharp move higher in recent days. The index is now extremely positive, and it is one of its largest movements in almost 4 years. This indicates that XRP supply is becoming tighter on Binance.

Ultimately, XRP is showing a clear split between its technical and on-chain signals.

While negative momentum and increasing short interest indicate that there is more downside to come, market outflows, whale accumulation, and reduced Binance supply suggest that selling pressure may be losing steam. If these supply patterns remain, then those shorting the market hoping for a fall to $1 may be caught off guard, turning the current bearish setup into a potential bear trap.

Rising XRP shorts and weak price action point to a possible drop toward $1.

Whale buying and exchange outflows suggest supply is tightening, raising the risk of a bear trap.

#5244. US Bank Regulator Issues First AI Framework as Banks Battle Rogue Agents

US Bank Regulator Issues First AI Framework as Banks Battle Rogue Agents

The U.S. Office of the Comptroller of the Currency issued its first supervisory framework for artificial intelligence in banking today, telling examiners to focus less on how powerful an AI system is and more on what happens when it makes a mistake.

The OCC's framework addresses that very concern, although the regulator has not linked its issuance to those specific incidents.

In a section covering operational resilience, the agency warns that external agents operated by customers, fintechs, or other businesses could create risks for banks even when the banks don't control those systems.

It suggests examiners consider measures including identity verification, delegated permissions, transaction limits, monitoring, and the ability to suspend or revoke access.

The guidelines aim to tackle the dilemma of how to let customers use AI assistants to manage their finances without giving autonomous software unrestricted access to accounts that banks are already facing.

The new OCC guidance arrives less than two weeks after reports that major banks were discovering unauthorized third-party AI agents operating inside customer accounts, sometimes taking actions that appeared to go beyond users' instructions.

Morgan Stanley was blocking outside agents, while JPMorgan, Bank of America, and Citi were restricting access, according to an Axios report on October 22. Bank executives described the situation as "horrifying," raising questions about who controls an autonomous agent once it has access to someone's money.

The OCC's framework makes a distinction between an AI system's technical sophistication and the damage it could cause.

A simple agent authorized to initiate financial transactions may present greater risk than a more advanced model limited to writing internal documents, the regulator said.

Under the 22-page framework, examiners can consider the consequences of failure, whether an action can be reversed, and how much control humans retain over the system.

The agency outlines four illustrative risk tiers. Fully autonomous payment agents and real-time credit decision systems fall into the highest category, while basic internal productivity tools occupy the lowest.

Higher-risk systems may warrant tighter restrictions on autonomy, independent testing, and human approval before consequential actions can proceed.

The framework also addresses AI systems that continue operating while making harmful decisions. Suggested safeguards include circuit breakers, rate limits, restricted permissions, and emergency shutdown controls.

Those considerations are especially relevant to the outside agents described in Axios's earlier reporting, where banks struggled to distinguish legitimate customer-authorized software from systems operating beyond their intended permissions.

The OCC stressed that the document does not create new regulatory requirements or enforceable standards for banks.

Instead, it gives examiners a way to evaluate AI within existing operational, compliance, and cybersecurity risk categories. Banks are not required to establish a separate AI risk category.

The document uses the mandatory term "Super Intelligence," which the Donald Trump administration insists on using in place of “artificial intelligence,” but its scope includes conventional AI and “autonomous agents” rather than only hypothetical systems with intelligence beyond human capabilities.

The framework covers AI used in payments, fraud detection, customer service, and other banking operations. It also extends its risk considerations to external AI agents connecting through bank channels.

The OCC said it intends to update the guidance as the technology develops. For now, its illustrative risk scale puts autonomous payment agents in the highest-risk category, regardless of how well they perform in testing.
